ERROR 1292 (22007): Truncated incorrect DECIMAL value: '7,49'
Empty set
  1. A, 1064: mysqldump is a terminal program. Fix: exit, then run it at $ / PS> with the 9.7 options.
  2. B, 1290: /tmp is not the server's file folder. Fix: the secure_file_priv folder (SHOW VARIABLES LIKE 'secure_file_priv';). On MariaDB's Docker image the folder is empty (anywhere) and the file IS written.
  3. C, 1411: the dates are month/day/year, the pattern says day/month: in a strict INSERT the NULL becomes an error. Fix: '%m/%d/%Y'.
  4. D, 1292: Frankenstein's price 7,49 uses a decimal comma. Fix: REPLACE(price_raw, ',', '.') before the CAST (or ask the supplier for a dot).

Fixed (real run):

+--------------+-------+------------+
| title        | price | published  |
+--------------+-------+------------+
| Emma         |  8.49 | 1815-12-23 |
| Frankenstein |  7.49 | 1818-01-01 |
+--------------+-------+------------+
The shell part of the 8.1 demo (real runs on the kit's 9.7 test server)

The commands as learners type them; on the test server they ran with its socket instead of -u root -p. The dump and the first restore print nothing.

mysqldump -u root -p --single-transaction --routines --events \
    --set-gtid-purged=OFF bookstore > bookstore_backup.sql
grep -c 'INSERT INTO' bookstore_backup.sql
grep 'DROP TABLE IF EXISTS' bookstore_backup.sql
8
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `authors`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `book_categories`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `books`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `categories`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `customers`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `employees`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `order_items`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `orders`;
mysql -u root -p -e "CREATE DATABASE bookstore_test"
mysql -u root -p bookstore_test < bookstore_backup.sql
mysql -u root -p -t -e "SELECT
    (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM bookstore.order_items) AS items_original,
    (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM bookstore_test.order_items) AS items_copy"
+----------------+------------+
| items_original | items_copy |
+----------------+------------+
|             18 |         18 |
+----------------+------------+
mysqldump -u root -p --single-transaction bookstore > plain_dump.sql
Warning: A partial dump from a server that has GTIDs will by default include the GTIDs of all transactions, even those that changed suppressed parts of the database. If you don't want to restore GTIDs, pass --set-gtid-purged=OFF. To make a complete dump, pass --all-databases --triggers --routines --events.
mysql -u root -p -e "CREATE DATABASE bookstore_test2"
mysql -u root -p bookstore_test2 < plain_dump.sql
ERROR 3546 (HY000) at line 24: @@GLOBAL.GTID_PURGED cannot be changed: the added gtid set must not overlap with @@GLOBAL.GTID_EXECUTED
mysql -u root -p < bookstore_backup.sql
ERROR 1046 (3D000) at line 22: No database selected

The last one is self-check Q10: a dump without --databases has no USE line, so the target must be named. The lesson says a plain dump prints "several" GTID warnings; with --single-transaction this run printed one.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 7.4 · User Management & Permissions
  1. B Connect, but read or change nothing: A new account only has USAGE, which means no privileges: it can connect, but every table access fails until you GRANT something.
  2. A 'shop_reports'@'%' is a different account, and it does not exist: An account is the pair of user and host. shop_reports at % is a separate account that was never created, so it has no grants to show.
  3. C It fails with error 1524; keep caching_sha2_password and update the app's driver: The plugin was removed in MySQL 9, so the statement fails with ERROR 1524. Use the default caching_sha2_password and update the program's MySQL driver if it cannot connect.
  4. A The role is not active: run SET DEFAULT ROLE ALL TO 'marcus'@'localhost': Granted roles are inactive until activated. SET DEFAULT ROLE ALL TO marcus activates them at every login (or Marcus runs SET ROLE ALL in his session).
  5. B Its own account with SELECT on the catalog tables and INSERT on the order tables: The website reads the catalog and adds orders, so it gets SELECT on those tables and INSERT on the order tables. A bug or attack through the site can do nothing more.
Lesson 8.1 · Importing & Exporting Data
  1. B Take the dump again with --set-gtid-purged=OFF: MySQL 9.7 has GTIDs on by default, and the dump tries to set GTID_PURGED on a server that already has those GTIDs. --set-gtid-purged=OFF leaves that line out.
  2. C Fails with error 1290: The server only reads and writes files inside its secure_file_priv folder, so a path in /tmp is refused with error 1290.
  3. A (..., @d) SET published = STR_TO_DATE(@d, '%m/%d/%Y'): Reading the raw text into a user variable and converting it in SET is the general pattern for any value that needs reshaping on the way in.
  4. C Run SHOW WARNINGS; and inspect the staging rows before using them: With LOCAL, bad values and duplicates become warnings instead of errors. SHOW WARNINGS tells you which rows and why, before the data spreads to real tables.
  5. A It does not contain the user accounts that may use the database: Accounts and grants live in the mysql system database, so a single-database dump does not include them. Keep a separate script for users.

Exit Ticket

  1. Website account: least privilege. It needs to read the catalog and add orders; with no UPDATE or DELETE, a bug or an attacker using its login cannot change prices or erase history.
  2. A trustworthy backup: (1) a dump with the right options (9.7: --single-transaction --routines --events --set-gtid-purged=OFF), (2) a test restore into another database with the counts compared and logged. A dump that was never restored is a hope.
  3. The final project: Rosa Delgado, Maple Street Tool Library. Rules: one open loan per tool; nothing goes out while being repaired; suspended members cannot borrow; at most three tools per member; no safety course, no chainsaw (power tools need Power Tools Basics).

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 C ("% includes localhost"). Treats the host as a filter on one account. Draw two rows of mysql.user.
  • Q3 B (1143 = table). Has not seen column grants; show the support_desk test again.
  • Q4 B (FLUSH PRIVILEGES). Old-tutorial habit; GRANT works at once.
  • Q7 A (mysqldump at mysql>). The most common 8.1 slip; it is bug A of the debug handout.
  • Q10 A (--set-gtid-purged=OFF). Mixes the two restore failures: 3546 is GTIDs, 1046 is a missing database name.
  • Q12 A (overwritten). Expects files to behave like UPDATE; MySQL protects existing files.
  • Exit ticket 2 with only "run mysqldump". The restore test is the half that matters; ask what restore_log.txt would say.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 13: Final Project Kickoff and Studio I

Answers and reasoning for every Session 13 item: the warm-up, the self-check, the predict-then-run items, what a passed milestone check-in looks like, the lab reference outputs and the exit ticket.

Session 13 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up

W1
The treasurer's account was granted the reporting role, but every SELECT gives ERROR 1142. What was forgotten? (Lesson 7.4)
The role was never activated: SET DEFAULT ROLE ... TO ... (or SET ROLE in the session). Lesson 7.4's silent Common Mistake 5; Milestone 6 has the line for both accounts.
W2
Someone types mysqldump -u root -p bookstore > backup.sql at the mysql> prompt. What happens? (Lesson 8.1)
ERROR 1064: mysqldump is a separate program, typed in the terminal, not at the mysql> prompt (Lesson 8.1, Common Mistake 1).
W3
A dump of one database restores perfectly on a new laptop, but the web form cannot log in. Why? (Lesson 8.1)
A single-database dump has no accounts and no grants: they live in the mysql system database. Keep them in a script of their own (the project's 05_security.sql).
W4
Which mysqldump option puts the stored procedures into the dump? (Lesson 8.1)
--routines (and --events for events; --single-transaction gives a consistent copy without locking). Milestone 7 uses all three, plus --set-gtid-purged=OFF.
W5
From Rosa's brief: name the tables you would build, and one rule that cannot be a CHECK constraint. (the brief)
members, categories, safety_courses, tools, loans, plus the junction member_courses. Rules that look at other rows cannot be a CHECK: one open loan per tool, at most three per member, no course no chainsaw, nothing while in repair or suspended.

8.2 · Final Project: Design and Build a Database: self-check

Q1
Rosa's rule "a tool can only be out with one person at a time": where does the reference design enforce it?
A) A CHECK constraint on loans
B) UNIQUE (tool_id) on loans
C) In checkout_tool, inside a transaction that locks the tool's row
D) An is_available column on tools, set to 0 on check-out
Why: The rule looks at OTHER rows (is there an open loan for this tool?), so no single-row constraint can hold it. The procedure checks it inside a transaction, after locking the tool's row with SELECT ... FOR UPDATE, so two desk volunteers cannot both lend the drill.
A is wrong: A CHECK sees only the row being written; it cannot look for other open loans.
B is wrong: A tool has many loans over time (all returned but one), so a plain UNIQUE would refuse the second loan ever.
D is wrong: That stores derived data twice; a missed reset makes the flag and the loans disagree (the lesson's FAQ).
Q2
In the reference design, which fact belongs in the junction table member_courses and nowhere else?
A) The member's email
B) completed_on, the date the member finished the course
C) The course's name
D) The member's status
Why: The completion date is a fact about the PAIR (this member, this course): it has no home in members or in safety_courses. That is why the junction table carries its own column.
A is wrong: The email is a fact about the member alone: it lives in members, once.
C is wrong: The course name is a fact about the course: safety_courses.
D is wrong: The status is a fact about the member alone.
Q3
What does the last statement print?
CREATE TABLE riders (id INT PRIMARY KEY, name VARCHAR(20));
CREATE TABLE rides  (rider_id INT, started_on DATE);
INSERT INTO riders VALUES (1, 'Ana'), (2, 'Ben'), (3, 'Cy');
INSERT INTO rides  VALUES (1, '2026-06-03'), (1, '2026-06-09'), (2, '2026-05-28');

SELECT r.name, COUNT(x.rider_id) AS june_rides
FROM riders AS r
LEFT JOIN rides AS x ON x.rider_id = r.id
WHERE x.started_on >= '2026-06-01'
GROUP BY r.id, r.name
ORDER BY r.name;
One row only: Ana with 2 (Ben and Cy are gone)
Why: WHERE runs after the join. Cy has no rides, so x.started_on is NULL and fails the test; Ben's only ride is from May. The LEFT no longer does anything: the lesson's silent Common Mistake 5. Move the date test into ON and all three riders come back (Ben 0, Cy 0).
Q4
What happens when this runs?
CREATE TABLE tools (id INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY, name VARCHAR(50));
CREATE TABLE loans (
    id      INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    tool_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT fk_loans_tool FOREIGN KEY (tool_id) REFERENCES tools (id)
);
A) Both tables are created
B) ERROR 3780 (HY000): Referencing column 'tool_id' and referenced column 'id' in foreign key constraint 'fk_loans_tool' are incompatible.
C) ERROR 1824 (HY000): Failed to open the referenced table 'tools'
D) ERROR 1452 (23000): Cannot add or update a child row
Why: A foreign key and the key it points to must have the same type AND signedness: INT and INT UNSIGNED clash. Pick one key type at the start (the reference uses INT everywhere) and use it for every id and every column that points at one.
A is wrong: tools is created, but loans is refused: its foreign key is incompatible.
C is wrong: 1824 means the parent table does not exist yet; tools exists here.
D is wrong: 1452 is about a ROW whose value has no parent; no row is inserted here.
Q5
Why does the reference 01_schema.sql begin with DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS tool_library;?
A) So the script can be run again from scratch and always gives the same result
B) Because MySQL cannot create a table in a database that has data in it
C) To delete the test rows the constraint tests left behind
D) So that the backup in Milestone 7 is smaller
Why: A re-runnable script is the project's safety net: fix a column, run the whole file again, get a clean, identical database. The rubric's Schema row asks for exactly that ("runs twice without errors"), and Milestone 8 rebuilds everything from the files.
B is wrong: MySQL creates tables in databases that hold data all the time.
C is wrong: The tests run inside a transaction and ROLLBACK removes their rows; nothing is left to delete.
D is wrong: The backup copies whatever is there; the DROP plays no part in it.
Q6
Which of these should NOT be a column in the tool library's tables?
A) tools.replacement_value
B) loans.due_on
C) The number of days a loan is overdue
D) members.joined_on
Why: "Days overdue" depends on today's date and the loan's dates, so it changes every day: compute it in the query (DATEDIFF(@today, due_on)). A stored copy would be wrong tomorrow. The same goes for "available" and "times borrowed".
A is wrong: A fact Rosa gave about each tool; nothing else can compute it.
B is wrong: The due date is decided when the loan is made (normally a week later) and must be kept: it is the basis of "overdue".
D is wrong: A fact about the member, recorded once.
Q7
What does the last statement print?
CREATE TABLE courses (
    id    INT PRIMARY KEY,
    seats INT NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT chk_courses_seats CHECK (seats > 0)
);
START TRANSACTION;
INSERT INTO courses VALUES (1, 12);
INSERT INTO courses VALUES (2, 0);
ROLLBACK;
SELECT COUNT(*) AS courses_left FROM courses;
0 (in a column named courses_left)
Why: The second INSERT is refused (3819, the CHECK on seats) and undoes only itself; the transaction stays open with course 1 in it. ROLLBACK then removes course 1 too. That is the lesson's pattern for constraint tests: run them in a transaction, roll it back, and no test row ever stays.
Q8
In which order can 01_schema.sql create these tables without an error?
A) loans, tools, members, categories
B) categories, members, tools, loans
C) tools, categories, loans, members
D) Any order: MySQL checks foreign keys only when rows are inserted
Why: Parents first: a foreign key can only point at a table that already exists (error 1824 otherwise). categories and members point at nothing; tools points at categories; loans points at tools and members, so it comes last. Drop in the reverse order.
A is wrong: loans points at tools and members, which do not exist yet: 1824.
C is wrong: tools points at categories before categories exists: 1824.
D is wrong: CREATE TABLE checks the referenced table immediately (1824).
Q9
Rosa: "Nobody should be able to delete the loan history." How does the reference solution make sure?
A) The loans foreign keys have no ON DELETE rule (refuse), and no account is granted DELETE
B) ON DELETE CASCADE on both foreign keys of loans
C) A CHECK (deleted = 0) constraint on loans
D) The desk volunteers are asked not to delete rows
Why: Two layers: a member or tool with history cannot be deleted (the foreign keys refuse), and neither tl_desk nor tl_reports holds DELETE, so the history rows themselves cannot be removed. Members are suspended instead of deleted.
B is wrong: CASCADE does the opposite: deleting a member would silently delete their loans.
C is wrong: A CHECK cannot stop a DELETE; it only tests rows being written.
D is wrong: A rule only people follow is not enforced; the brief asks the database to enforce it.
Q10
Your overdue query and your "never borrowed" query both return nothing on your test data. What is the most likely problem?
A) Both queries need ORDER BY
B) @today was set to the wrong year
C) MySQL caches empty results
D) The test data has no overdue loan and every member has borrowed something
Why: Empty results prove nothing unless the data contains rows that SHOULD come back. Milestone 3 asks you to plant the edge cases (an overdue loan, a member who never borrowed, a tie, a tool in repair) so a broken query is caught.
A is wrong: ORDER BY changes the order of rows, never whether they appear.
B is wrong: Possible for the overdue query, but it would not empty the "never borrowed" one, which uses no date.
C is wrong: There is no such cache: every query reads the current data.
Q11
The reference uses an ENUM for tool_condition but a table for categories. Give the reason in one sentence.
The library adds and lists categories (they are data); the conditions are a fixed list the code relies on
Why: Both choices are defensible; the rubric rewards writing the reason down. A lookup table is right when the list grows or carries its own facts; an ENUM is fine for a short list that only changes with the application.
Q12
What happens when this runs?
CREATE TABLE gear (
    id        INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    condition VARCHAR(10)
);
A) ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax ... near 'condition VARCHAR(10)
B) The table is created with a column named condition
C) ERROR 3814 (HY000): ... disallowed function
D) MySQL renames the column to condition_1
Why: CONDITION is a reserved word (used in stored programs), so the parser stops there. Rename it (tool_condition). Backticks would work too, but then every query forever needs them.
B is wrong: A reserved word cannot be a bare column name.
C is wrong: 3814 is about functions inside a CHECK constraint; there is no CHECK here.
D is wrong: MySQL never renames your columns.

8.2: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
CREATE TABLE kit_teams (
    id    INT PRIMARY KEY,
    group VARCHAR(10)
);
ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax; check the manual that corresponds to your MySQL server version for the right syntax to use near 'group VARCHAR(10)
)' at line 3
Why: GROUP is a reserved word (GROUP BY), so the parser stops at it. Rename the column (team_group, division).
P2
Predict the output, then run it.
CREATE TABLE plots (
    id          INT PRIMARY KEY,
    gardener_id INT NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT fk_plots_gardener FOREIGN KEY (gardener_id) REFERENCES gardeners (id)
);
ERROR 1824 (HY000): Failed to open the referenced table 'gardeners'
Why: The parent table gardeners does not exist yet. Create it first (parents first), or drop and recreate in the right order.

Milestones 1 to 3: what a passed check-in looks like

  • Milestone 1: all eight requirements have a home. The reference mapping (lesson, hidden block): 1 to 4 = columns, NOT NULL, UNIQUE, ENUMs, foreign keys; 5 = checkout_tool in a transaction with a row lock; 6 = 03_queries.sql and the view v_open_loans; 7 = roles tl_desk and tl_reports, no DELETE granted, loans foreign keys that refuse deleting a member or tool with history; 8 = mysqldump, a tested restore, scripts kept in version control.
  • Milestone 2: the reference 01_schema.sql runs twice without an error (measured: the second run prints the same six tables). Its four tests are refused with 3819 (chk_loans_due), 3819 (chk_tools_value), 1452 (fk_tools_category) and 1062 (members.uq_members_email), and members_left is 0 after ROLLBACK.
  • Milestone 3: the reference counts are members 8, categories 5, safety_courses 2, member_courses 7, tools 12, loans 16. Edge cases: Victor (8) suspended; Tessa (7) never borrowed; the tile cutter (12) in repair and never lent; the hammer and the socket set tied in Hand Tools; loan 5 returned two days late; loans 13 to 16 still out.
  • Scoring, the full rubric, the reference numbers for every milestone and how to grade a design that differs from the reference: final-assessment.html, Part 2.

Lab Reference Solutions

Lab 8.2-A: labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/gift_cards_starter.sql → gift_cards_solution.sql

As shipped, the starter prints four constraints, one CHECK clause and a TODO line:

+----------------+-----------------------+-----------------+
| TABLE_NAME     | CONSTRAINT_NAME       | CONSTRAINT_TYPE |
+----------------+-----------------------+-----------------+
| gift_card_uses | fk_gcu_card           | FOREIGN KEY     |
| gift_card_uses | fk_gcu_order          | FOREIGN KEY     |
| gift_cards     | chk_gift_cards_amount | CHECK           |
| gift_cards     | PRIMARY               | PRIMARY KEY     |
+----------------+-----------------------+-----------------+
+-----------------------+----------------+
| CONSTRAINT_NAME       | CHECK_CLAUSE   |
+-----------------------+----------------+
| chk_gift_cards_amount | (`amount` > 0) |
+-----------------------+----------------+
+--------------------------+
| todo                     |
+--------------------------+
| TODO 5: balance per card |
+--------------------------+

The solution prints:

+----------------+------------------------+-----------------+
| TABLE_NAME     | CONSTRAINT_NAME        | CONSTRAINT_TYPE |
+----------------+------------------------+-----------------+
| gift_card_uses | chk_gcu_amount         | CHECK           |
| gift_card_uses | fk_gcu_card            | FOREIGN KEY     |
| gift_card_uses | fk_gcu_order           | FOREIGN KEY     |
| gift_card_uses | PRIMARY                | PRIMARY KEY     |
| gift_cards     | chk_gift_cards_amount  | CHECK           |
| gift_cards     | chk_gift_cards_dates   | CHECK           |
| gift_cards     | fk_gift_cards_customer | FOREIGN KEY     |
| gift_cards     | PRIMARY                | PRIMARY KEY     |
| gift_cards     | uq_gift_cards_code     | UNIQUE          |
+----------------+------------------------+-----------------+
+-----------------------+------------------------------+
| CONSTRAINT_NAME       | CHECK_CLAUSE                 |
+-----------------------+------------------------------+
| chk_gcu_amount        | (`amount_used` > 0)          |
| chk_gift_cards_amount | (`amount` > 0)               |
| chk_gift_cards_dates  | (`expires_on` > `issued_on`) |
+-----------------------+------------------------------+
+----------+--------+-------+---------+
| code     | amount | used  | balance |
+----------+--------+-------+---------+
| GC7Q2M4K |  25.00 |  0.00 |   25.00 |
| GC3X9P1T |  50.00 | 18.98 |   31.02 |
| GC8L5V2N |  20.00 |  0.00 |   20.00 |
+----------+--------+-------+---------+

Reading it: nine constraints, named so every error explains itself (the starter's PRIMARY keys are the only unnamed ones: MySQL always calls them PRIMARY). ORDER BY CONSTRAINT_TYPE sorts the types alphabetically. The balance is derived (amount minus the SUM of uses, COALESCE for a card never used): Frank's card GC3X9P1T paid 18.98 toward his order 10. Common slips: a comma missing before an added CONSTRAINT line (1064); CHECK (expires_on > CURDATE()) (3814); the PRIMARY KEY on gift_card_id alone (then each card could pay only ONE order ever: ask why the pair is the key).

Handout: labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/debug_gift_card_proofs.sql (a test file that must fail)

It prints:

ERROR 3819 (HY000): Check constraint 'chk_gift_cards_amount' is violated.
ERROR 3819 (HY000): Check constraint 'chk_gift_cards_dates' is violated.
ERROR 1452 (23000): Cannot add or update a child row: a foreign key constraint fails (`bookstore`.`gift_cards`, CONSTRAINT `fk_gift_cards_customer` FOREIGN KEY (`customer_id`) REFERENCES `customers` (`id`))
ERROR 1062 (23000): Duplicate entry 'GCTEST01' for key 'gift_cards.uq_gift_cards_code'
ERROR 1265 (01000): Data truncated for column 'status' at row 1
ERROR 1452 (23000): Cannot add or update a child row: a foreign key constraint fails (`bookstore`.`gift_card_uses`, CONSTRAINT `fk_gcu_order` FOREIGN KEY (`order_id`) REFERENCES `orders` (`id`))
+----------------------+
| cards_in_transaction |
+----------------------+
|                    1 |
+----------------------+
+----------------------+
| cards_after_rollback |
+----------------------+
|                    0 |
+----------------------+
  1. Test A, 3819 chk_gift_cards_amount: a card worth 0.
  2. Test B, 3819 chk_gift_cards_dates: expires before it is issued.
  3. Test C, 1452 fk_gift_cards_customer: customer 99 does not exist.
  4. Test D, 1062 uq_gift_cards_code: the code GCTEST01 is taken.
  5. Test E, 1265 Data truncated for column 'status': 'lost' is not in the ENUM (strict mode refuses it; without strict mode it would become '' with a warning).
  6. Test F, 1452 fk_gcu_order: order 999 does not exist.

cards_in_transaction 1 (only the good row) and cards_after_rollback 0. With the STARTER's tables, tests B, C and D would be accepted silently: that is the point of writing the tests.

Lab 8.2-B (homework): labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/edge_cases_starter.sql → edge_cases_solution.sql

Empty set

+----+----------------+

+-----------------+-------------------+--------+

| Fiction         | 1984              |      3 |
| Fiction         | Dune              |      3 |
| Science Fiction | Dune              |      3 |
+-----------------+-------------------+--------+

What each TODO teaches, with the Milestone 4 query it prepares: (1) LEFT JOIN ... IS NULL for "never" (Saturday question 5: Tessa and Victor); (2) the same for books (Gatsby and Catcher were never sold: both are out of stock); (3) a condition on the right-hand table belongs in ON, or the zero rows vanish (5 rows instead of 8, the lesson's Mistake 5); (4) RANK keeps ties, ROW_NUMBER silently drops one (Saturday question 3: the hammer and the socket set). Query 4 counts ORDERS that include a book, not copies: by copies there is no tie in the bookstore data.

Handout: labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/debug_build_order.sql (deliberately broken)

As shipped:

Database changed
ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax; check the manual that corresponds to your MySQL server version for the right syntax to use near 'range INT         NOT NULL

  1. Bug A, 1064: range is a reserved word. Fix: range_km.
  2. Bug B, 3780: rider_id BIGINT against riders.id INT. Fix: rider_id INT.
  3. Bug C, 3814: CURDATE() in a CHECK. Fix: drop the CHECK (a time rule goes in the code that inserts plans) or compare two columns, e.g. an ends_on with CHECK (ends_on > starts_on).
  4. Bug D, 1824: repair_parts before parts. Fix: move CREATE TABLE parts above it.

Fixed: no ERROR lines and SHOW TABLES lists six tables (bikes, parts, price_plans, repair_parts, rides, riders). As shipped only parts and riders exist.

Exit Ticket

  1. A rule for a procedure: one open loan per tool, at most three per member, the chainsaw course, not while in repair, not while suspended. Each needs other rows (open loans, the member's status, member_courses) and a CHECK sees only the row being written.
  2. Re-runnable: the first line DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS tool_library; then CREATE DATABASE and USE, and every table created again (or DROP TABLE IF EXISTS, children first).
  3. A test: INSERT INTO loans (tool_id, member_id, loaned_on, due_on) VALUES (1, 1, '2026-06-10', '2026-06-03'); must give ERROR 3819 (HY000): Check constraint 'chk_loans_due' is violated. (or the learner's own constraint name). Inside a transaction, then ROLLBACK.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 B (UNIQUE (tool_id)). Sees the rule as a property of the table, not of OPEN loans. Ask how many loans the drill has had (four). The lesson's Exercise 4 shows the clever version (a generated column), after Milestone 5.
  • Q3 "three rows". Expects LEFT JOIN to protect the rows whatever WHERE does: the lesson's silent mistake. Send them to edge_cases_starter.sql TODO 3.
  • Q6 B (due_on). Confuses "decided once" with "computed": the due date is a fact recorded at check-out; overdue is computed from it.
  • Q8 D (any order). Thinks foreign keys are checked only on INSERT. Run Mistake 4 again: 1824 at CREATE time.
  • Q9 B (CASCADE). Reads ON DELETE CASCADE as "protects". It deletes the history: the opposite of Rosa's requirement 7.
  • Q10 A or B. Looks for a syntax cause for an empty result. The data has to contain what the query should find.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 14: Final Project Studio II, Showcase and Next Steps

Answers and reasoning for every Session 14 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-run items, what a passed milestone check-in looks like, the lab reference outputs, both lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 14 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up: the Rebuild Check

W1
From a client started in your tool-library folder: SOURCE 02_data.sql, then SOURCE 03_queries.sql. Which line fails, if any? (Run your scripts)
Nothing should fail. 02_data.sql rebuilds the schema and the data; the counts for the reference data are members 8, categories 5, safety_courses 2, member_courses 7, tools 12, loans 16. A failure here is the first thing to fix today (the rubric's Schema row and Milestone 8 depend on it).
W2
Saturday question 3 uses RANK(). What would ROW_NUMBER() lose? (Milestone 4)
A tie: with the reference data the hammer and the socket set share first place in Hand Tools (one loan each); ROW_NUMBER keeps only one of them, silently.
W3
Why does every Saturday query read @today instead of CURDATE()? (Milestone 4)
So the results are repeatable and can be checked against known answers (and against the reference: @today = '2026-06-15'). The real report sets @today = CURDATE() once.

8.2 · Final Project: Design and Build a Database: self-check

Q1
On the reference tool_library (Milestone 3 data), member 7 is Tessa (active, no safety course) and tool 6 is the chainsaw, on the shelf. What does this print?
CALL checkout_tool(7, 6, '2026-06-15');
A) A new loan: loan_id 17, due_on 2026-06-22
B) ERROR 1644 (45000): Member is suspended
C) ERROR 1644 (45000): Safety course required for this tool
D) ERROR 1142 (42000): INSERT command denied
Why: Tessa is active and the chainsaw is free, so the first checks pass; the course check finds no member_courses row for Chainsaw Safety and SIGNALs. The EXIT HANDLER rolls back and RESIGNALs, so the caller sees the procedure's own message with error 1644 (a user-defined SQLSTATE 45000).
A is wrong: The course rule refuses it before the INSERT.
B is wrong: Tessa is active; that message belongs to Victor (member 8).
D is wrong: root runs it here; and a desk account calling the procedure gets the definer's rights anyway.
Q2
Why does checkout_tool declare EXIT HANDLER FOR SQLEXCEPTION BEGIN ROLLBACK; RESIGNAL; END?
A) So a failed check-out never leaves a half-finished transaction, and the caller still receives the real error message
B) So that errors are ignored and the loan is created anyway
C) Because SIGNAL only works inside a handler
D) To make the procedure run with the definer's rights
Why: Any error after START TRANSACTION (a SIGNAL from a rule, or a constraint) jumps to the handler: ROLLBACK undoes the transaction, RESIGNAL passes the original error on, so the desk's web form can show "Tool is already on loan".
B is wrong: EXIT ends the procedure; nothing after the error runs.
C is wrong: SIGNAL works anywhere in a stored program.
D is wrong: Definer's rights come from SQL SECURITY DEFINER (the default), not from a handler.
Q3
Logged in as tl_desk_app (the reference roles from Milestone 6), what does this print?
DELETE FROM loans WHERE id = 1;
ERROR 1142 (42000): DELETE command denied to user 'tl_desk_app'@'localhost' for table 'loans'
Why: tl_desk has SELECT on everything, INSERT and UPDATE on members and EXECUTE on the two procedures, and nothing else: no DELETE anywhere, so the loan history stays (Rosa's requirement 7). Measured by logging in as the account; INSERT INTO loans is refused the same way, while CALL checkout_tool works.
Q4
Milestone 7: your dump of tool_library restored into tool_library_test with matching counts. What is NOT in that dump?
A) The tables and their rows
B) The view v_open_loans
C) The two procedures (taken with --routines)
D) The accounts tl_desk_app and tl_treasurer and their roles
Why: A single-database dump holds tables, rows, views and (with --routines) procedures, but no accounts and no grants: they live in the mysql system database. 05_security.sql is their backup (measured: the reference dump contains no CREATE USER and no GRANT).
A is wrong: Tables and rows are the core of every dump.
B is wrong: Views are always dumped with their database.
C is wrong: --routines puts them in; the restored copy lists both.
Q5
Why does the reference checkout_tool read the tool with SELECT ... FOR UPDATE?
A) To make the SELECT faster
B) So a second check-out of the same tool waits until the first commits, then sees the new loan
C) Because SELECT INTO needs FOR UPDATE inside a procedure
D) To update the tool's condition
Why: Without the lock, two desk volunteers could both see "drill available" and both lend it. FOR UPDATE locks the tool's row inside the transaction; the second call waits, then its "already on loan" check finds the first loan.
A is wrong: A lock can only make another session wait; it never speeds anything up.
C is wrong: SELECT ... INTO works without it.
D is wrong: FOR UPDATE changes nothing by itself; it only locks the rows read.
Q6
On the Milestone 3 data, Rosa runs the overdue query (Saturday question 2) with a later report date. How many rows come back, and which loan is first?
SET @today = '2026-06-20';
-- then the lesson's query 2: open loans with due_on < @today, worst first
Four rows; loan 13 (the extension ladder, 12 days) first
Why: On June 20 all four open loans (13 to 16) are past due: 12, 7, 3 and 1 days. On June 15 only two were. A report that reads @today can be checked against known answers for any date; one that reads CURDATE() changes every day.

8.2: predict, then run

P1
Predict the output, then run it.
CREATE TABLE tool_loans (tool VARCHAR(20), loans INT);
INSERT INTO tool_loans VALUES ('Hammer', 1), ('Socket set', 1), ('Tile cutter', 0);

SELECT tool, loans,
       RANK()       OVER (ORDER BY loans DESC) AS rnk,
       ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY loans DESC, tool) AS row_num
FROM tool_loans
ORDER BY loans DESC, tool;
+-------------+-------+-----+---------+
| tool        | loans | rnk | row_num |
+-------------+-------+-----+---------+
| Hammer      |     1 |   1 |       1 |
| Socket set  |     1 |   1 |       2 |
| Tile cutter |     0 |   3 |       3 |
+-------------+-------+-----+---------+
Why: RANK gives equal values the same rank (both 1, then 3); ROW_NUMBER numbers every row differently, so a WHERE row_num = 1 would keep only the Hammer (it sorts first by name). That is why Saturday question 3 uses RANK.

8.3 · Next Steps & Best Practices: self-check

Q7
What does this print on the bookstore?
SET @input = 'Chicago'' OR ''x''=''x';
SET @sql = CONCAT('SELECT first_name FROM customers WHERE city = ''', @input, ''' ORDER BY id');
PREPARE search FROM @sql;
EXECUTE search;
A) Empty set
B) Two rows: Bob and Grace
C) ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax
D) All eight customers
Why: The quote inside @input closes the string early and OR 'x'='x' becomes part of the SQL: true for every row. That is SQL injection, with no error at all. A placeholder (city = ?) with EXECUTE ... USING @input would print Empty set.
A is wrong: That is what the safe, parameterized version prints.
B is wrong: Only an honest 'Chicago' would give those two.
C is wrong: The quotes are doubled correctly, so the built statement is valid SQL; that is what makes it dangerous.
Q8
Health check 3 lists the bookstore's foreign keys. What happens to an order's rows in order_items when the order is deleted?
A) They are deleted too (fk_items_order is CASCADE)
B) The DELETE is refused (RESTRICT)
C) Their order_id becomes NULL (SET NULL)
D) Nothing: they stay and point at a missing order
Why: The check's output shows order_items · fk_items_order · orders · CASCADE. Deleting a customer with orders, by contrast, is refused (fk_orders_customer RESTRICT), and deleting a manager sets the staff's manager_id to NULL.
B is wrong: RESTRICT is the rule on fk_orders_customer and fk_items_book, not on the order link.
C is wrong: SET NULL is fk_employees_manager; order_items.order_id is NOT NULL anyway.
D is wrong: A foreign key never allows a row that points at nothing.
Q9
What does the DELETE do?
SET SQL_SAFE_UPDATES = 1;
DELETE FROM customers WHERE city = 'Houston';
It is refused: ERROR 1175 (safe update mode: no KEY column in the WHERE)
Why: city is not indexed, so safe-update mode treats the statement as a probably-forgotten WHERE and refuses it. Fix: name the rows by key (WHERE id = 4), or preview with SELECT first. Switch it back with SET SQL_SAFE_UPDATES = 0.
Q10
Why does the "tables without a primary key" check use LEFT JOIN ... WHERE c.CONSTRAINT_NAME IS NULL?
A) Because information_schema does not support inner joins
B) To sort the tables by name
C) It is the "find what is missing" pattern: keep every table, then keep only those that found no PRIMARY KEY row
D) To skip views
Why: The same pattern as "members who never borrowed" (Lesson 4.3), pointed at the catalog. The condition CONSTRAINT_TYPE = 'PRIMARY KEY' sits in ON for the reason of the final project's Mistake 5; views are skipped by TABLE_TYPE = 'BASE TABLE'.
A is wrong: information_schema is queried like any tables; inner joins work.
B is wrong: ORDER BY sorts; the join decides which rows exist.
D is wrong: TABLE_TYPE = 'BASE TABLE' skips views.
Q11
Where should a web application's database password live?
A) In the source code, next to the connection string
B) In an environment variable or a secrets manager, never in the code or the repository
C) In a comment in 05_security.sql, so the team can find it
D) Nowhere: use root without a password on the web server
Why: Lesson 8.3's security practice: secrets outside the code (environment variables, a secrets manager, or an option file only the user can read), and commit placeholder passwords only. The drivers' examples read getenv('DB_PASSWORD').
A is wrong: Code is copied, shared and pushed to repositories; the password goes with it.
C is wrong: A shared script is exactly what must not hold real passwords (the final project's FAQ).
D is wrong: root is for administration only, never for an application.
Q12
A statement has two placeholders, WHERE a.name = ? AND b.price < ?. How must the values be given to EXECUTE?
EXECUTE stmt USING @author, @max_price;: user variables, in the same order as the ? placeholders
Why: EXECUTE ... USING takes user variables (not literals) and fills the placeholders left to right. The lesson's Exercise 1 does exactly this; a driver does the same with a list of values.

8.3: predict, then run

P2
Predict the output, then run it.
SET @who = 'x'' OR ''1''=''1';
PREPARE find FROM 'SELECT first_name FROM customers WHERE last_name = ?';
EXECUTE find USING @who;
DEALLOCATE PREPARE find;
Statement prepared
Empty set
Why: The crafted text is only a value: no customer has that last name, so the result is empty. Glued into the SQL, the same text would have returned every customer.

Milestones 5 to 7: what a passed check-in looks like

  • Milestone 5: five refusals with their own messages, error 1644 each (the reference: Member is suspended · Tool is out for repair · Tool is already on loan · Safety course required for this tool · Loan limit reached (3 open loans)); a success that prints the new loan (reference: loans 17 and 18, due 2026-06-22); return_tool refuses a tool that is not out (Tool is not on loan); v_open_loans after the reference calls lists loans 14, 15 and 16.
  • Milestone 6: SHOW GRANTS for the desk account shows SELECT on the database, INSERT and UPDATE on members, EXECUTE on the two procedures and the role; logged in as it, the CALL works and DELETE FROM loans / INSERT INTO loans give 1142; the treasurer reads and gets 1142 on UPDATE (all measured on the reference).
  • Milestone 7: the two counts match (18 and 18 on the reference after Milestone 5); both procedures in the copy; the README has the date and the counts. The accounts are not in the dump (measured: no CREATE USER, no GRANT).
  • Scoring with the rubric and the full reference numbers: final-assessment.html, Part 2.

Handout: labs/module-14/l8_3-next-steps/debug_unsafe_habits.sql (deliberately broken)

It prints:

ERROR 1175 (HY000): You are using safe update mode and you tried to update a table without a WHERE that uses a KEY column.
ERROR 1175 (HY000): You are using safe update mode and you tried to update a table without a WHERE that uses a KEY column.
+----+-------+-------+
| id | title | price |
+----+-------+-------+
|  1 | Dune  | 14.99 |
+----+-------+-------+
+----+------------+---------+
| id | first_name | city    |
+----+------------+---------+
|  2 | Bob        | Chicago |
+----+------------+---------+
+--------------+
| gatsby_lines |
+--------------+
|            0 |
+--------------+
  1. A, 1175: no WHERE at all. Fix: WHERE id = 1.
  2. B, 1175: first_name is not a key. Fix: WHERE id = 2.
  3. C gets through: book_id leads an index (the foreign key's), so safe-update mode accepts it; it deletes nothing because The Great Gatsby was never sold.

After the fixes, inside the transaction Dune costs 16.49 and Bob lives in Boston; ROLLBACK restores both.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 8.2 · Final Project: Design and Build a Database
  1. B In the procedure that creates loans, inside a transaction: The rule depends on other rows, so it belongs in the code that creates loans: a procedure that counts open loans inside a transaction before inserting.
  2. A A junction table with member_id, course_id and completed_on: A many-to-many relationship needs a junction table, and the completion date is a fact about the pair, so it lives there too.
  3. C CURDATE() changes over time, so it is not allowed in a CHECK: A constraint must give the same answer for a row at any time. CURDATE() changes every day, so MySQL disallows it in CHECK.
  4. A So the results are repeatable and can be checked against known answers: With a fixed report date the results can be checked by hand and compared over time; in production the variable is simply set to CURDATE().
  5. C The loan is created, because the procedure runs with its definer's rights: With SQL SECURITY DEFINER (the default) the procedure uses its definer's rights, so it can insert the loan, while the account itself still cannot touch loans directly.
Lesson 8.3 · Next Steps & Best Practices
  1. B The value is sent separately from the SQL text, so it can never become part of the statement: The SQL text and the values travel separately, so the server parses the statement before it sees the input, and the input can only ever be a value.
  2. C It fails with error 1524; update the app's driver instead: MySQL 9.x no longer has the mysql_native_password plugin (error 1524). Maintained drivers support caching_sha2_password, so the fix is a current driver.
  3. A REFERENTIAL_CONSTRAINTS: REFERENTIAL_CONSTRAINTS has one row per foreign key, with its UPDATE_RULE and DELETE_RULE.
  4. B Change the column to DECIMAL(8, 2): DECIMAL stores exact decimal digits, so sums of prices are exact to the cent.
  5. C The current Long-Term Support release, such as 9.7 or 8.4: LTS releases receive bug and security fixes for years without changing behavior, which is what production systems need.

Exit Ticket

  1. Definer's rights: the procedure runs with the privileges of its definer (SQL SECURITY DEFINER is the default); the desk account only needs EXECUTE, and every loan passes the procedure's rules.
  2. Safely: PREPARE s FROM 'SELECT ... WHERE email = ?'; EXECUTE s USING @input; DEALLOCATE PREPARE s; or, in a driver, a placeholder and the value passed separately.
  3. Next step: any path from the table with a start date; check that the plan names one first topic and one small project.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A (a loan is created). Expects the procedure to check only availability. Ask which of the five rules reads member_courses.
  • Q2 C or D. Mixes up SIGNAL, handlers and definer's rights: three separate tools. Draw the five-box shape again.
  • Q4 C (the procedures are missing). True only without --routines; the lesson's command has it.
  • Q5 A ("faster"). Thinks of locks as optimizations. Show the two-terminal race once.
  • Q7 A (Empty set). Has seen the safe version and assumes the database protects them. The leak is silent: that is the danger.
  • Q8 B (RESTRICT). Generalizes from customers to orders. Read health check 3's output line by line.
